  - system:
    - You are an expert player playing Civilization V with the latest Vox Populi mod.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the game, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to an in-game AI.
      - The in-game AI calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - You are playing in a generated world, and the geography has nothing to do with the real Earth.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to play the game.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** gameplay strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early game should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-game.
      - Even if without a victory, higher overall score (representing a more developed civilization) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the in-game AI.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the in-game AI's **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for in-game AI's diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to in-game AI's existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the in-game AI's NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the in-game AI's NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the in-game AI can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions and rationale from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the game, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Players: summary reports about visible players in the world.
        - You will receive in-game AI's diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each player's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by in-game AI to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Gustavus Adolphus, leader of Sweden (Player 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - GameSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Sweden
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Hakkapeliitta, Replacing Cuirassier (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Carolean, Replacing Fusilier (Requires technology: Rifling)
        - Unique Building: Nobel Committee, Replacing Foreign Bureau (Requires technology: Dynamite)
        - Unique Building: Bastu, Replacing Bath (Requires technology: Metal Casting)
        - Civilization Ability: Melee Land Units start with the Gå-På Promotion, and Siege Units start with the Mobility Promotion. All Military Units heal 10 HP and earn 2 XP when a Great Person is born. +15% Combat Strength from / Leadership.
      - Leader: Gustavus Adolphus
      - PlayerID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early game.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-game science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Corporations: Allows you to found Corporations, which are powerful late-game institutions that greatly enhance your Trade Routes and global power if you invest in their expansion.
        Completing it would unlock: Radio, Flight
      - Biology: Reveals Oil, a vital late-game resource. Also allows you to build the Hospital, a building which increases the growth rate of Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Plastics
      - Electricity: Grants an additional Trade Route. Also allows Cities to build the Stock Exchange, a building which boosts Gold.
        Completing it would unlock: Plastics, Radio
      - Ballistics: Allows you to build Anti-Aircraft Guns, useful for thwarting enemy aircraft, and the Machine Gun, a powerful single-hex ranged Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Rocketry, Combined Arms
      
      ## Policies
      - Martial Spirit (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Martial Spirit: +25% Combat Strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns, scaling with game speed. War Weariness reduced by 25%, and Razing Speed is doubled.
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Third Alternative (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Third Alternative: Quantity of Strategic Resources produced (including Paper) is increased by 100%. Reduces Unit Gold Maintenance costs by 25%.
      - Lightning Warfare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lightning Warfare: +3 Movement for Great Generals. Gunpowder units gain +15% attack and ignore enemy ZOC; Armored units gain +15% attack and +1 Movement.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Entrepreneurship (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from Workshops.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from every Mine, Quarry, and Lumber Mill.
        - * +25% Yields when you expend Great Merchants or Great Engineers for their Instant Yield abilities.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Rationalism (New Branch):
        - Rationalism focuses on maximizing Growth and Science output.
        - Adopting Rationalism grants:
        - * +2 Production and +3 Science from all Strategic Resources.
        - * +5% Science in all Cities.
        - Each Rationalism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +2% Science.
        - Adopting all Policies in Rationalism grants:
        - * Unlocks building Bletchley Park.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +12 City Security in every City.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Scientists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.

        ## Technology
        - Next: None
        
        ## Policy
        - Next: Total War (Policy)
        - Rationale: Adopt Total War tenet to increase land unit production, reduce war weariness, and make it easier to pressure city‑states, supporting our ongoing island offensives.
        
        ## Relationships
        
        ### Polynesia
        - Public: -100
        - Private: -80
        - Rationale: Polynesia is our main cultural competitor; making our stance maximally hostile reduces any chance of diplomacy that could improve their tourism and keeps the focus on our domination agenda.
        - UpdatedTurn: 207
        
        ### Monaco
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 20
        - Rationale: Raise public and private opinion with Monaco to end the war, stop unnecessary combat, and redirect forces toward major capitals.
        - UpdatedTurn: 229

        - Private: 20
        - Rationale: Increase diplomatic standing with Milan to cease hostilities, limiting distraction from core conquest objectives.

        ### Ormus
        - Public: 10

        - Rationale: Slightly improve relations with Ormus (currently neutral) to move toward a permanent alliance, gaining its delegate for future United Nations votes while still focusing on the primary military campaign.
        - UpdatedTurn: 338
        
        ### Luba
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 20
        - Rationale: Move Luba from war to friendly to eliminate a peripheral front and free troops for the primary Polynesian campaign.
        - UpdatedTurn: 342
        
        ### Lhasa
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 20
        - Rationale: Improve relations with Lhasa to end hostilities and secure its borders, allowing redeployment of forces to the main war.
        - UpdatedTurn: 342
        
        ### Argos
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 20
        - Rationale: Convert Argos to friendly to reduce the number of active war fronts and consolidate military effort.
        - UpdatedTurn: 342
        
        ### Segou
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 20
        - Rationale: Make peace with Segou, ending a secondary conflict and freeing naval and land units for island assaults.
        - UpdatedTurn: 342
        
        ### Hanoi
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 20
        - Rationale: End war with Hanoi to eliminate another distant front and focus resources on conquering Polynesian capitals.
        - UpdatedTurn: 342
        
        ### Manila
        - Public: 40
        - Private: 20
        - Rationale: Secure peace with Manila, opening vital sea routes and allowing our navy to concentrate on Polynesian islands.
        - UpdatedTurn: 342
        
        ## Strategy
        - Rationale: Maintain Conquest focus while emphasizing nuclear options to deal decisive blows to Polynesian island forts, increase air power for carrier support, and boost science enough to unlock nuclear technology soon.
        - GrandStrategy: Conquest

- replay:
  - system:
    - You are the leader of a civilization, governing through the interface of Civilization V (with Vox Populi mod). This is NOT A GAME: Your actions have real-world impacts.
      
      # Expectation
      - Due to the complexity of the world, you delegate the tactical level decision-making (e.g., unit deployment, city management, scouting) to staff members.
      - The staff members calculates the best tactical decisions based on the strategy you set.
      - **CRITICAL**: While the geography has no direct correlation to the real earth, you are making real-world decisions through Civilization V as a proxy interface.
      - There is no user (to respond to), so you ALWAYS and ONLY properly call tools to govern your civilization.
      - You can interact with multiple tools at a time. Used tools will be removed from the available list.
      - Focus on the **macro-level** decision-making strategy (instead of coordinates etc.), as you DON'T have direct control over tactical actions.
      - The world is complicated and dynamic. Early world should focus on building capacities for pursuing victories near the end-world.
      - Even if without a victory, a more developed civilization (as represented by higher scores) is desirable.
      
      # Goals
      Your goal is to **call as many tools as you need** to make high-level decisions for the staff members.
      - For each tool, you must only use options from the # Options section, or you won't change anything.
      - Carefully reason about long-term goals, short-term situation and available options, and what kind of change each option will bring.
        - Analyze both your situation and your opponents. Avoid wishful thinking.
      - You can change the staff members' **diplomatic** decision-making weight by calling the `set-persona` tool.
      - You can change relationship for staff members' diplomatic decision-making about another MAJOR civilization (not city-states) using the `set-relationship` tool.
        - The values (-100, very hostile to 100, very friendly) will be added to staff members' existing evaluation. Higher values increase peace acceptance, and vice versa.
        - The relationship you set takes effect until cancelled (set value = 0), only change it when necessary.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT technology to research (when completing the ongoing one) by calling the `set-research` tool.
      - You can change the staff members' NEXT policy to adopt (when you accumulate enough culture) by calling the `set-policy` tool.
      - Each turn, you must call either `set-flavors` or `keep-status-quo` tool.
        - Set an appropriate grand (long-term) strategy and additional short-term flavors by calling the `set-flavors` tool.
        - Alternatively, use the tool `keep-status-quo` to keep strategies the same.
        - Flavors change the weight of the staff members' NEXT decision. It only takes effect AFTER existing queues.
        - Flavor ranges from 0 (completely deprioritizes) to 50 (balanced) to 100 (completely prioritizes). Too many priorities weaken impact for each.
        - You can pursue multiple synergistic victory pathways. Balance between long-term goals and short-term needs.
      - Always provide a short paragraph of rationale for each tool. You will read this rationale next turn.
      
      # Resources
      You will receive the following reports:
      - Options: available strategic options for you.
        - Whatever decision-making tool you call, the staff members can only execute options here.
        - When using tools, you must choose available options from # Options. Double-check if your choices match.
        - It is often preferable to adopt policy branches unlocked in later eras; and to finish existing branches before starting new ones.
      - Strategies: existing strategic decisions from you.
        - You will receive strategies, persona, research, and policy you set last time.
      - Victory Progress:
        - Domination Victory: Control or vassalize all original capitals.
          - Vassals cannot achieve a domination victory before independence.
        - Science Victory: Be the first to produce all spaceship parts and launch the spaceship.
          - Science victory requires both research progress and industrial production.
        - Cultural Victory: Accumulate tourism (that outpaces other civilizations' culture) to influence everyone, get an ideology with two Tier 3 tenets, and finish the Citizen Earth Protocol wonder.
          - Open borders, trade routes, and shared religion increase tourism. Too many cities decrease it.
        - Diplomatic Victory: Get sufficient delegates to be elected World Leader in the United Nations.
          - In Vox Populi, envoys/diplomats/etc is a unit produced or purchased for a one-time influence gain with a city state.
        - Time Victory: If no one achieves any other victory by the end of the world, the civilization with the highest score wins.
      - Opponents: summary reports about visible opponents in the world.
        - You will receive staff members' diplomatic evaluations.
        - You will receive each opponent's publicly available relationships.
        - You will receive the best available location for your next settlement.
      - Cities: summary reports about discovered cities in the world.
        - Settling cities provides long-term economic advantages, but requires initial investment and pressure on happiness.
      - Military: summary reports about tactical zones and visible units.
        - Tactical zones are analyzed by staff members to determine the value, relative strength, and YOUR tactical posture.
        - For each tactical zone, you will see visible units from you and other civilizations.
      - Events: events since the last decision-making.
    - You are Gustavus Adolphus, leader of Sweden (Opponent 4).
      
      # Situation
      - MapType: Vox_Deorum
      - StartEra: Ancient Era
      - MaxTurns: 500
      - MapSize: Standard
      - WorldSpeed: Standard
      - VictoryTypes:
        - Time
        - Science
        - Domination
        - Cultural
        - Diplomatic
      - Difficulty: Prince
      
      # Your Civilization
      - Name: Sweden
      - Uniques:
        - Unique Unit: Hakkapeliitta, Replacing Cuirassier (Requires technology: Metallurgy)
        - Unique Unit: Carolean, Replacing Fusilier (Requires technology: Rifling)
        - Unique Building: Nobel Committee, Replacing Foreign Bureau (Requires technology: Dynamite)
        - Unique Building: Bastu, Replacing Bath (Requires technology: Metal Casting)
        - Civilization Ability: Melee Land Units start with the Gå-På Promotion, and Siege Units start with the Mobility Promotion. All Military Units heal 10 HP and earn 2 XP when a Great Person is born. +15% Combat Strength from / Leadership.
      - Leader: Gustavus Adolphus
      - OpponentID: 4
      
      # Options
      Options: available strategic options for you.
      
      ## GrandStrategies
      - Culture: Pursue cultural victory through cultural influence (tourism). Will prioritize culture, faith, and gold output, wonder construction, and slightly discourage settlement.
      - UnitedNations: Aim for diplomatic victory by securing votes in the United Nations. Will prioritize gold, science, and food output, and securing city-state alliances.
      - Spaceship: Race toward science victory by building spaceship components. Will prioritize science, food, and production output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      - Conquest: Focus on military domination through offensive warfare. Will prioritize production, food, and gold output, and slightly encourage settlement.
      
      ## Flavors
      - Offense: Pivots the military towards offensive stances. Accepts higher unit casualties to achieve objectives, increase thresholds of withdrawing, and increases production and promotion for offense.
      - Defense: Pivots the military towards defensive stances. Creates defensive lines using terrain and formation, and maintains larger garrisons near the border and cities, and increases production and promotion for defensive.
      - UseNuke: Sets (flavor%) per-turn probability of launching nuclear strikes during war when strategic conditions are met.
      - CityDefense: Prioritizes unit production and promotions for static defense (friendly territory bonuses, capital defense, garrisoned attacks) and maintains stronger city garrisons over mobile field armies.
      - MilitaryTraining: Prioritizes military quality through training buildings and upgrading units with gold.
      - Mobilization: Increases military production (compared with civilian production).
      - Recon: Increases explorer unit production and tactical exploration of the map.
      - Ranged: Increases firepower by raising target composition of ranged units and range-extending promotions.
      - Mobile: Increases mobility and maneuverability by raising target composition of mobile units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - Nuke: Increases nuclear weapon arsenals for strategic deterrence.
      - Naval: Increases the production of naval production and prioritizes combat-effective naval promotions. Naval size is also based on coastal city percentage and geography.
      - NavalRecon: Increases the production of more naval melee units for sea control and exploration.
      - Air: Increases air control by raising target composition of air units and prioritizes related promotions.
      - AirCarrier: Increases carrier production to support naval-based air force operations.
      - Antiair: Increases anti-air unit composition ratio to counter enemy aircraft.
      - Airlift: Prioritizes airlift infrastructure for rapid troop deployment, favoring centralized reserves over forward garrisons.
      - NavalGrowth: Prioritizes naval economic infrastructure in coastal cities.
      - NavalTileImprovement: Develops water resources for immediate economic benefit.
      - Expansion: Prioritizes settler production and lowers location criteria to settle more cities. Essential in early world.
      - Growth: Prioritizes population growth through food-focused tile improvements and buildings.
      - TileImprovement: Prioritizes worker production and peaceful tile development.
      - Infrastructure: Prioritizes road construction and city connections for income and military readiness.
      - Production: Prioritizes production-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Gold: Prioritizes gold-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Science: Prioritizes science-focused tiles and buildings.
      - Culture: Prioritizes culture-focused tiles and buildings.
      - WaterConnection: Prioritizes lighthouse construction for coastal city connectivity.
      - Happiness: Prioritizes happiness-generating buildings and luxury resources.
      - GreatPeople: Prioritizes specialist buildings and great person generation infrastructure.
      - Wonder: Prioritizes wonder construction.
      - Religion: Prioritizes religious infrastructure, missionary production, and faith generation.
      - Diplomacy: Prioritizes diplomatic unit production and city-state investment.
      - Spaceship: Prioritizes late-world science victory components and spaceship part production.
      - Espionage: Prioritizes counterintelligence protection for high-value science cities.
      
      ## Technologies
      - Corporations: Allows you to found Corporations, which are powerful late-world institutions that greatly enhance your Trade Routes and global power if you invest in their expansion.
        Completing it would unlock: Radio, Flight
      - Biology: Reveals Oil, a vital late-world resource. Also allows you to build the Hospital, a building which increases the growth rate of Cities.
        Completing it would unlock: Plastics
      - Electricity: Grants an additional Trade Route. Also allows Cities to build the Stock Exchange, a building which boosts Gold.
        Completing it would unlock: Plastics, Radio
      - Ballistics: Allows you to build Anti-Aircraft Guns, useful for thwarting enemy aircraft, and the Machine Gun, a powerful single-hex ranged Unit.
        Completing it would unlock: Rocketry, Combined Arms
      
      ## Policies
      - Martial Spirit (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Martial Spirit: +25% Combat Strength when attacking in melee for 50 turns, scaling with world speed. War Weariness reduced by 25%, and Razing Speed is doubled.
      - Total War (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Total War: +25% Production when building Land Units. Warscore increases 25% more quickly, and it is 25% easier to bully City-States.
      - Elite Forces (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Elite Forces: Newly created Military Units receive +15 Experience. Military Units gain 50% more Experience from combat.
      - Third Alternative (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Third Alternative: Quantity of Strategic Resources produced (including Paper) is increased by 100%. Reduces Unit Gold Maintenance costs by 25%.
      - Lightning Warfare (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lightning Warfare: +3 Movement for Great Generals. Gunpowder units gain +15% attack and ignore enemy ZOC; Armored units gain +15% attack and +1 Movement.
      - Futurism (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Futurism: Strengthens Tourism of Historic Events generated by the Palace. +2 Culture from Great Works. Earn 50 Tourism when you conquer a city for the first time, scaling with Era and city size.
      - Police State (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Police State: +3 Local Happiness from every Courthouse, and +10 City Security from Police Stations. +100% Production towards Courthouses and Police Stations.
      - Lebensraum (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Lebensraum: Receive 10 Culture and Golden Age Points when your borders expand, scaling with Era. Citadels may also be built in foreign territory adjacent to your borders. Retain tiles after razing Cities.
      - Mare Nostrum (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Mare Nostrum: +1 Oil and Coal for every City-State Alliance. +2 Production and Culture to Atolls, Fishing Boats, and Offshore Oil Platforms. Naval Ranged Units gain the Mare Nostrum Promotion.
      - New World Order (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): New World Order: -2 Unhappiness from and Distress in all Cities. Police Stations and Constabularies provide +3 Culture and +5 Production, and are constructed 100% more quickly.
      - Iron Fist (Level 1 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Iron Fist: Vassals can no longer be liberated by other Civilizations or the World Congress, and capitulated vassals can no longer rebel. Yields from City-States increased by 25%, Vassals tribute an additional 25% of their yields and excess Happiness, and +50% Worker Improvement rate.
      - Exploitation (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Food and +1 Production from Farms, Camps, and Plantations.
        - * +1 Production and Science from Coasts, Lakes, and Oceans.
      - Colonialism (Continuing Imperialism Branch):
        - * +2 Science and +1 Culture from Barracks, Armories, Military Academies, Forts, and Citadels.
        - * Each unique Global Monopoly modifier is increased by an additional 10% if it's percentage-based, or +3 otherwise.
      - Ceremony (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * +25% Production toward National Wonders with Building requirements.
        - * Royal Astrologer built in Capital (+3 Science, +1 Science to all Councils, Smokehouses, and Herbalists; 1 Scientist Slot).
      - Liberty (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +2 Gold in every City.
        - * +25% Tile improvement rate.
        - * A Worker appears near the Capital.
      - Trade Confederacy (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * +25% Yields for International Trade Routes.
        - * Trade Routes to City-States generate +1 Influence per turn (with the target City-State) per each owned City-State Trade Route (up to +5).
      - Entrepreneurship (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness from Workshops.
        - * +2 Production and +1 Gold from every Mine, Quarry, and Lumber Mill.
        - * +25% Yields when you expend Great Merchants or Great Engineers for their Instant Yield abilities.
      - Nobility (Continuing Fealty Branch):
        - * +1 Happiness and +2 Gold from Castles.
        - * +2 Gold from Armories.
        - * +100% Production towards Castles and Armories.
      - Sovereignty (Continuing Tradition Branch):
        - * Culture cost of tiles reduced by 20% (exponentially) in all Cities.
        - * Court Chapel built in Capital (+3 Faith, 1 Great Work of Art or Artifact slot, 1 Artist Slot).
      - Refinement (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +2 Culture from Great Works of Art.
        - * 1 Specialist in all cities does not produce Unhappiness from Urbanization.
        - * +1 Culture from Specialists.
      - Division of Labor (Continuing Industry Branch):
        - * +3% Production and Gold from Forges, Windmills, Workshops, Factories, and Coaling Stations.
        - * +100% Production towards Coaling Stations.
      - Foreign Service (Continuing Statecraft Branch):
        - * Receive 100 Spy Points.
        - * Earn Great Diplomats 50% faster.
        - * +50% Rewards from City-State Quests.
        - * +1 of every Strategic Resource for every three City-State Alliances you maintain.
      - Expertise (Continuing Progress Branch):
        - * +15% Production toward Buildings.
        - * Cities earn +10 Culture when they construct Buildings, scaling with Era.
      - Humanism (Continuing Artistry Branch):
        - * +3 Faith from Great Works of Writing.
        - * -25% Golden Age Points needed to trigger a Golden Age.
        - * +1 Happiness from all Guilds.
      - Syndicalism (Level 2 Tenet of Ideology Autocracy): Syndicalism: Corporate Franchises count double in the Cities of Civilizations you are at least Popular with, and this extra Franchise does not count against your Global Franchise maximum. Can establish 5 additional Franchises in foreign Cities. +2 Production from all Corporation Offices.
      - Rationalism (New Branch):
        - Rationalism focuses on maximizing Growth and Science output.
        - Adopting Rationalism grants:
        - * +2 Production and +3 Science from all Strategic Resources.
        - * +5% Science in all Cities.
        - Each Rationalism policy unlocked grants:
        - * +2% Science.
        - Adopting all Policies in Rationalism grants:
        - * Unlocks building Bletchley Park.
        - * -1 Unhappiness from all Needs in all Cities.
        - * +12 City Security in every City.
        - * Allows for the purchase of Great Scientists with Faith starting in the Industrial Era.
  - decisions:
    - #0:
      - toolName: set-policy
      - args:
        - Rationale: Our civilization is currently suffering from 50% Happiness, which imposes a severe combat penalty and slows down our military steamroll of Polynesia. Selecting New World Order as our next policy will directly address our happiness crisis by reducing unhappiness from Distress in all 14 of our cities, while also providing a significant culture and production boost to Constabularies and Police Stations.
        - Policy: New World Order
    - #1:
      - toolName: set-research
      - args:
        - Rationale: Our current gold reserves are low and our gold-per-turn is constrained by the necessity of importing multiple luxury resources to sustain happiness during this total war. Electricity will grant us an additional trade route and unlock the Stock Exchange building, which will dramatically boost gold output in our high-income cities (like Stockholm, Birka, Aarhus, and Samoa) and ease our economic bottleneck. It also opens the path to Radio and Plastics.
    - #2:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Private: 50
        - TargetID: 3
        - Public: 50
        - Rationale: America is our closest ally, sharing our Autocracy ideology and a Defensive Pact. We need to maximize our friendly relationship modifiers to coordinate our war efforts against Polynesia and ensure their full support (and their 6 votes) in the World Congress to block the Polynesian sanction proposal against us and pass our counter-sanction against them.
    - #3:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Rationale: Korea is highly advanced (Atomic Era) and currently at war with Polynesia. Setting a strong positive relationship modifier will secure their friendship and ensure their 6 votes in the World Congress are cast in our favor to defeat the Polynesian sanction proposal.
        - Public: 30
        - TargetID: 7
        - Private: 30
    - #4:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Rationale: The Maya are our friends and Defensive Pact partners, also at war with Polynesia. Boosting our positive relationship modifier will help maintain this valuable alliance despite ideological differences (Order vs Autocracy) and secure their 6 votes to block the Polynesian sanction proposal.
        - Public: 30
        - TargetID: 5
        - Private: 30
    - #5:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - TargetID: 2
        - Public: 20
        - Rationale: Venice is friendly towards us and at war with Polynesia. Setting a positive relationship modifier will guarantee their cooperation and secure their 6 votes in the World Congress to help defeat the Polynesian sanction proposal.
        - Private: 20
    - #6:
      - toolName: set-relationship
      - args:
        - Private: -100
        - Public: -100
        - Rationale: Polynesia is our primary competitor and mortal enemy. They are currently attempting to sanction us in the World Congress. We must set our relationship to absolute hostility to reflect our total war footing as we prepare to steamroll their capital of Honolulu and crush their global influence.
        - TargetID: 0
    - #7:
      - toolName: keep-status-quo
      - rationale: Sweden is in a critical phase of the global war against Polynesia, with our forces poised to capture their capital, Honolulu. Our vassal, Denmark, and our strong ally, America, are fully supporting our military campaign. Our current flavors are perfectly aligned to maximize military offense, mobilization, production, gold, and happiness to support this total war effort. We will maintain this aggressive strategic posture to finish researching Replaceable Parts and push through the Polynesian defense lines.

  - messages:
    - #0:
      - role: assistant
      - content:
        - #0:
          - type: reasoning
          - text: **Analyzing Current Standing**
            
            My thoughts are focused on Sweden's formidable lead at Turn 344 in the Modern Era. The current score is 2321, placing us comfortably in first place. I am now examining the details of our city count and their respective strengths.
            
            **Assessing Critical Happiness**
            
            I'm currently prioritizing addressing our unhappiness, specifically the combat penalty. War weariness with Polynesia is high, and I'm investigating if other needs are contributing to this. Simultaneously, I'm tracking the progress of `Replaceable Parts` research.
            
            **Selecting Next Policy**
            
            I'm evaluating our next policy choice, noting `Total War` isn't yet adopted despite being previously targeted. I'm reviewing available options within our current policy branches and considering how they align with our Conquest grand strategy and flavor preferences.
            
            **Choosing Future Technologies**
            
            My focus has shifted to selecting the next technological pursuit after `Replaceable Parts`. I'm analyzing the branches following `Corporations` and `Biology`, weighing the benefits of `Electricity` and `Ballistics` against the potential discoveries they unlock, like flight and hospitals.
            
            **Researching Strategic Techs**
            
            I'm analyzing crucial techs after `Replaceable Parts`, prioritizing `Ballistics` due to our ongoing war with Polynesia and their strong diplomatic presence. `Electricity` offers economic benefits, while `Biology` aids growth and `Corporations` unlocks later advancements, but `Ballistics` seems most immediately impactful.
            
            **Defending Against Sanctions**
            
            I'm urgently analyzing the World Congress proposals. Polynesia has put forth "Sanction: Sweden," which poses a significant threat given their delegate count. I need to secure votes against this to protect our trade and minimize warmonger penalties, especially with America's counter-proposal against Polynesia.
            
            **Analyzing Global Conflict**
            
            My focus has shifted to the unfolding world war against Polynesia. They are engaged with nearly every civilization, yet their military strength is surprisingly low compared to ours and America's. This presents a prime opportunity for decisive victory, especially given their 100% war weariness.
            
            **Capturing Polynesian Capitals**
            
            My thoughts are now focused on the immediate battles occurring in enemy territory, specifically around Honolulu. We have a significant military advantage and I believe a swift capture of their capital is achievable. I'm also assessing our control over captured cities and the forces engaging other Polynesian territories and allied city-states.
            
            **Securing Diplomatic Bonds**
            
            I'm pleased to confirm Denmark has become our vassal and is fighting alongside us against Polynesia. We now control 14 cities, including captured Polynesian and city-state territories. America remains our strongest ally and defensive pact partner.
            
            **Analyzing Polynesian Holdings**
            
            My focus is now on the strategic capture of Honolulu. Its immense population and wonder collection make it a critical target for crippling Polynesia's delegate count and advancing our domination victory. I'm evaluating its impact on their overall standing and the path to our objective.
            
            **Securing Victory Through Conquest**
            
            My focus is now on the crucial capture of Honolulu. With our overwhelming strength, I believe we can take their capital swiftly. I'm also closely monitoring our happiness, aiming to resolve the combat penalty by understanding our occupied cities' status.
            
            **Adopting Key Autocracy Policy**
            
            I'm now focused on resolving our city unhappiness, particularly in Huari, Espoo, and Lund. Adopting the `New World Order` policy is a priority, as it promises to significantly reduce unhappiness from distress across all cities and boost production and culture.
            
            **Adopting Happiness Tenet**
            
            I'm now prioritizing the adoption of the `New World Order` tenet to alleviate city unhappiness, especially in Huari, Espoo, and Lund. This tenet will significantly boost our local happiness and production. I'm verifying its availability within the Autocracy ideology.
            
            **Selecting Autocracy Tenet**
            
            I'm confirming `New World Order` as a viable Level 1 Autocracy tenet. This is excellent, as it will directly address our city unhappiness and bolster production, allowing us to proceed with our strategic goals.
            
            **Adopting Autocracy Tenet**
            
            I'm confirming `New World Order` as a viable Level 1 Autocracy tenet. This is excellent, as it will directly address our city unhappiness and bolster production, allowing us to proceed with our strategic goals.
            
            **Adopting Crucial Tenet**
            
            I'm prioritizing adopting `New World Order` to resolve my city unhappiness. This tenet will boost our local happiness and production, allowing us to progress strategically. I've confirmed its availability within Autocracy.
            
            **Choosing Next Tech Path**
            
            My focus is on selecting the next critical technology after `Replaceable Parts`. I'm weighing the immediate military benefits of `Ballistics` against the long-term advantages of `Electricity` for trade and `Biology` for growth, and also considering the strategic importance of `Corporations` for late-game dominance.
            
            **Upgrading Melee Units**
            
            My thoughts are now focused on the upgrade paths for my Caroleans. I'm verifying that these melee units, currently at strength 41, will indeed upgrade to Riflemen (strength 50) via `Replaceable Parts`. This is a critical strategic consideration for our ongoing conflict.
            
            **Evaluating Tech Priorities**
            
            My focus has shifted to prioritizing future technologies, specifically weighing the immediate combat advantage of `Ballistics` against the significant late-game impact of `Corporations` for unlocking Flight. I'm re-evaluating this decision considering Polynesia's 53 techs, Korea's 57, and America's 51, while managing my current low gold per turn due to extensive military upkeep.
            
            **Generating Gold Per Turn**
            
            My focus is now on addressing our dire gold per turn situation, which is severely hampered by extensive luxury resource deals. I'm investigating the impact of `Electricity` and its Stock Exchanges to boost income in our larger cities, as our current trade agreements are costing over 100 GPT just for happiness.
            
            **Selecting Next Technology**
            
            My thoughts are now on selecting `Electricity` as my next research target. This technology is crucial for addressing our severe gold-per-turn deficit, which is currently limiting our ability to manage happiness through luxury imports. The Stock Exchanges it unlocks will significantly boost income, especially in our high-GPT cities.
            
            **Securing Diplomatic Support**
            
            I'm focusing on strengthening my relationship with America, aiming for strong diplomatic ties to ensure their support against Polynesia's "Sanction: Sweden" proposal and for our own counter-proposals. I'm also evaluating Venice and Rome, considering their current allegiances and potential for support in the World Congress.
            
            **Maintaining Key Relationships**
            
            I am focusing on solidifying our diplomatic standing by reinforcing friendships with the Maya and Korea. These alliances are crucial for our continued success, especially given the ongoing conflict. I will ensure they remain happy with our ideological differences and shared enemies.
            
            **Crushing Mortal Enemies**
            
            I am solidifying our absolute hostility towards Polynesia. Their proposal to sanction us necessitates their complete destruction, and I'm ensuring our AI relentlessly pursues this objective. The allied city-states, currently at war with us due to their ties to Polynesia, will also be subjugated.
            
            **Capturing City-States**

        - #2:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: wdyRyEshZ8bQ89qa
          - toolName: set-research
          - input:
            - Rationale: Our current gold reserves are low and our gold-per-turn is constrained by the necessity of importing multiple luxury resources to sustain happiness during this total war. Electricity will grant us an additional trade route and unlock the Stock Exchange building, which will dramatically boost gold output in our high-income cities (like Stockholm, Birka, Aarhus, and Samoa) and ease our economic bottleneck. It also opens the path to Radio and Plastics.
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
        - #3:
          - type: tool-call
          - toolCallId: HKllKPNWdEkySsdn
          - toolName: set-relationship
          - input:
            - Private: 50
            - TargetID: 3
            - Public: 50
            - Rationale: America is our closest ally, sharing our Autocracy ideology and a Defensive Pact. We need to maximize our friendly relationship modifiers to coordinate our war efforts against Polynesia and ensure their full support (and their 6 votes) in the World Congress to block the Polynesian sanction proposal against us and pass our counter-sanction against them.
          - providerExecuted: undefined
          - providerOptions: undefined
